argument: a multiple may have a single tropical root for all the roots of an
annulus, as $x^{2M}+\rho^{2M}$ and $x^{2m}+x+B$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:noangle} do, and then only integrality can separate
them.

Integrality does separate them when the roots of the annulus lie in a thin
sector, and that is the case of the register roots $a\pm p^bi$ with $|a|$
bounded.  The angle mechanism of Proposition~\ref{prop:noangle} puts $K$ roots
within an angle $\delta$ of a ray only at the price of degree about
$K/\delta$, and at Gaussian roots degree costs mass
(Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge}).  A multiple of smaller degree is handled by
the real theory, applied to the real polynomial
$r\mapsto\operatorname{Im}\bigl(e^{-i\theta}F(re^{i\psi})\bigr)$ along a ray
$\arg z=\psi$ next to the sector.  For $0<x\le\pi/2$ put
\[
  E(x)=\frac{4x}{\log2}\Bigl(1+\log\Bigl(1+\frac{\pi}{2x}\Bigr)\Bigr).
\]
$E$ is increasing, since
$\frac{d}{dx}\bigl(x(1+\log(1+\frac{\pi}{2x}))\bigr)=1+\log(1+t)-\frac t{1+t}>0$
with $t=\pi/(2x)$.

\begin{lemma}[Crossings next to a sector]\label{lem:sectorcount}
Let $B\in\mathbb C[x]$ be nonzero of degree $d$, let $\varphi\in\R$,
$0<\delta'\le\pi/2$ and $R>0$, and suppose that $B$ has no zero other than $0$
on the two rays $\arg z=\varphi\pm\delta'$, and at least $K$ zeros, counted
with multiplicity, in $\{z:|z|\ge R,\ |\arg z-\varphi|<\delta'\}$.  Then there are
$r_1\in[R/2,R)$ and $\psi\in\{\varphi-\delta',\varphi+\delta'\}$ such that for
every $\theta\in\R$ the real polynomial
\[
  h_\theta(r)=\operatorname{Im}\bigl(e^{-i\theta}B(re^{i\psi})\bigr)
  =\sum_{k=0}^d\operatorname{Im}\bigl(e^{-i\theta}b_ke^{ik\psi}\bigr)\,r^k
\]
has at least $K-dE(\delta')/\pi-1$ distinct zeros in $(r_1,\infty)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For $r>0$ let $\Gamma_r=\{re^{it}:|t-\varphi|\le\delta'\}$, traversed in the
direction of increasing $t$, and for a point $\beta$ with $|\beta|\ne r$ let
$\Delta_\beta(r)$ be the change of a continuous argument of $z-\beta$ along
$\Gamma_r$.  We claim
\[
  |\Delta_\beta(r)|\ \le\ \min\Bigl(2\pi,\ \frac{2\delta'r}{\bigl|r-|\beta|\bigr|}\Bigr).
\]
The second bound holds because $|d\arg(z-\beta)|\le|dz|/|z-\beta|$, the arc
has length $2\delta'r$, and $|z-\beta|\ge|r-|\beta||$ on it.  For the first:
if $|\beta|<r$, the argument of $re^{it}-\beta$ has derivative
$(r^2-\operatorname{Re}(\bar\beta re^{it}))/|re^{it}-\beta|^2>0$ and increases by
$2\pi$ over a full turn, so $0<\Delta_\beta(r)<2\pi$; if $|\beta|>r$, the
points $z-\beta$, $|z|=r$, lie in a closed disc not containing $0$, which is
seen from $0$ under an angle less than $\pi$, so $|\Delta_\beta(r)|<\pi$.

Next, for every $\varrho\ge0$ and $a>0$,
\[
  \int_a^{2a}\min\Bigl(2\pi,\frac{2\delta'r}{|r-\varrho|}\Bigr)\frac{dr}r
  \ \le\ 4\delta'\Bigl(1+\log\Bigl(1+\frac\pi{2\delta'}\Bigr)\Bigr)
  =E(\delta')\log2 .
\]
Indeed, the integrand is $\min(2\pi/r,2\delta'/|r-\varrho|)$.  Put
$\eta=\delta'a/\pi$.  Where $|r-\varrho|\le\eta$ it is at most $2\pi/a$ on a
set of measure at most $2\eta$, which contributes at most $4\delta'$.  Elsewhere
it is at most $2\delta'/|r-\varrho|$ on a set of measure at most $a$ on which
$|r-\varrho|>\eta$; such an integral is largest for the set
$\eta<|r-\varrho|\le\eta+a/2$, where it equals
$4\delta'\log(1+a/(2\eta))=4\delta'\log(1+\pi/(2\delta'))$.

Let $\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_d$ be the zeros of $B$ with multiplicity, and
$S(r)=\sum_l\min(2\pi,2\delta'r/|r-|\beta_l||)$.  By the last display,
$\int_a^{2a}S(r)\,dr/r\le dE(\delta')\log2$, while
$\int_a^{2a}dr/r=\log2$; so $S(r)\le dE(\delta')$ on a subset of $[a,2a]$ of
positive measure.  Taking $a=R/2$, choose $r_1\in[R/2,R)$ there, different from
every $|\beta_l|$; taking $a=M$ larger than every $|\beta_l|$, choose $r_2$ in
the same way in $[M,2M]$.  Since
$\arg B(z)=\arg b_d+\sum_l\arg(z-\beta_l)$, the change $\Delta_{\rm arc}(r_i)$
of $\arg B$ along $\Gamma_{r_i}$ satisfies
$|\Delta_{\rm arc}(r_i)|\le S(r_i)\le dE(\delta')$.

Let $\psi_\pm=\varphi\pm\delta'$ and let $\Delta_\pm$ be the change of
$\arg B(re^{i\psi_\pm})$ as $r$ increases from $r_1$ to $r_2$.  The region
$W=\{r_1<|z|<r_2,\ |\arg z-\varphi|<\delta'\}$ has no zero of $B$ on its
boundary and contains the $K$ zeros of the statement (their moduli are at
least $R>r_1$ and below $M<r_2$).  The argument principle along
$\partial W$, traversed counterclockwise, gives
\[
  2\pi K\ \le\ \Delta_-+\Delta_{\rm arc}(r_2)-\Delta_+-\Delta_{\rm arc}(r_1)
  \ \le\ |\Delta_-|+|\Delta_+|+2dE(\delta').
\]
Let $\psi$ be the ray with the larger $|\Delta_\pm|$, so that
$|\Delta_\psi|\ge\pi K-dE(\delta')$, and let $A(r)$ be a continuous argument of
$B(re^{i\psi})$ on $[r_1,r_2]$.  Fix $\theta$.  The open interval between
$A(r_1)$ and $A(r_2)$ has length $|\Delta_\psi|$, so it contains at least
$|\Delta_\psi|/\pi-1$ points of $\theta+\pi\mathbb Z$; by continuity each of
them equals $A(r)$ for some $r\in(r_1,r_2)$, and distinct points give distinct
$r$.  At such $r$ the number $e^{-i\theta}B(re^{i\psi})$ is real, so
$h_\theta(r)=0$.  This gives at least $|\Delta_\psi|/\pi-1\ge K-dE(\delta')/\pi-1$
distinct zeros of $h_\theta$ in $(r_1,r_2)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Thin sectors]\label{thm:thinsector}
Let $B\in\mathbb C[x]$ have degree $d$ and $|b_d|\ge1$, let $\varphi\in\R$,
$0<\delta\le\pi/4$ and $R\ge6$, and suppose that $B$ has at least $K$ zeros,
counted with multiplicity, in $\{z:|z|\ge R,\ |\arg z-\varphi|\le\delta\}$.  If
$d\,E(2\delta)\le\pi K/2$, then with $m=\lceil K/2\rceil-1$,
\[
  b_k(B)\ \ge\ |b_d|\Bigl(\frac R2-1\Bigr)^{m-k+1}\quad(1\le k\le m),
\]
\[
  \Lambda(B)\ \ge\ (m+1)\log|b_d|+\frac{m(m+1)}2\log\Bigl(\frac R2-1\Bigr),
\]
and $\frac{m(m+1)}2\ge\frac{K(K-2)}8$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Choose $\delta'\in(\delta,2\delta]$ such that no zero of $B$ lies on the rays
$\arg z=\varphi\pm\delta'$; all but finitely many values qualify.  The $K$
zeros lie in the open sector $|\arg z-\varphi|<\delta'$, and $E(\delta')\le
E(2\delta)$ because $E$ is increasing.  Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorcount} gives
$r_1\ge R/2$ and a ray $\psi$ such that every $h_\theta$ has at least
$K-dE(2\delta)/\pi-1\ge K/2-1$ distinct zeros in $(r_1,\infty)$, hence at least
$m$ of them, say $\varrho_1<\cdots<\varrho_m$, all larger than $R/2\ge3$.
Choose $\theta$ with $e^{-i\theta}b_de^{id\psi}=i|b_d|$.  Then $h_\theta$ is a
nonzero real polynomial of degree $d$ with leading coefficient $|b_d|$ and
coefficients $h_k=\operatorname{Im}(e^{-i\theta}b_ke^{ik\psi})$, so
$|h_k|\le|b_k|$, and it is divisible by $\prod_{i\le m}(x-\varrho_i)$ with
$\varrho_1\ge2$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:order} and Corollary~\ref{cor:mass} give
$b_k(h_\theta)\ge|b_d|\prod_{i=k}^m(\varrho_i-1)$ and
$\Lambda(h_\theta)\ge(m+1)\log|b_d|+\sum_{i=1}^mi\log(\varrho_i-1)$, and
$\varrho_i-1>R/2-1$.  The coefficientwise domination $|h_k|\le|b_k|$, with the
leading coefficients at the same position $d$, gives $b_k(B)\ge b_k(h_\theta)$
and $\Lambda(B)\ge\Lambda(h_\theta)$.  Finally $m+1\ge K/2$ and $m\ge K/2-1$.
\end{proof}

The theorem uses no integrality, and its degree hypothesis cannot be
dropped: $x^{2M}+\rho^{2M}$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:noangle} has about
$2\delta M/\pi$ roots within $\delta$ of the imaginary axis and
$b_2=0$, and its degree $2M$ exceeds $\pi K/(2E(2\delta))$.  At Gaussian roots
the large-degree case is paid for by Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge}.

\begin{theorem}[Thin Gaussian clusters]\label{thm:thingauss}
Let $\varphi\in\R$, $0<\delta\le\pi/4$ and $R\ge6$, and suppose that every
$\alpha_j$ has $|\alpha_j|\ge R$ and $|\arg\alpha_j-\varphi|\le\delta$.  Every
nonzero $F\in\mathbb Z[x]$ divisible by $P$ satisfies $\Lambda(F)\ge2K\log R$
and
\[
  \Lambda(F)\ \ge\ \min\Bigl\{\frac{K(K-2)}8\log\Bigl(\frac R2-1\Bigr),\
  \frac{\pi K\log(R/2)}{2E(2\delta)}\Bigr\},
  \qquad
  \frac{\pi}{2E(2\delta)}\ \ge\ \frac{\pi\log2}{16\,\delta\log(e\pi/(2\delta))}.
\]
If moreover some block $B$ of $F$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussblocks}) has
$\deg B\cdot E(2\delta)\le\pi K/2$, then
$b_k(F)\ge(R/2-1)^{m-k+1}$ for $1\le k\le m=\lceil K/2\rceil-1$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $B$ be a block of $F$, of degree $d$.  It is divisible by $P$, so it
vanishes at the $K$ distinct $\alpha_j$; $B(0)\ne0$, $|b_d|\ge1$,
$\Lambda(F)\ge\Lambda(B)$ and $b_k(F)\ge b_k(B)$.  If $dE(2\delta)\le\pi K/2$,
Theorem~\ref{thm:thinsector} applies to $B$ and gives the first term, and the
rows.  Otherwise $d>\pi K/(2E(2\delta))$, and Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge},
with $\rho_{\min}\ge R>2$, gives
$\Lambda(B)\ge d\log(\rho_{\min}/2)>\pi K\log(R/2)/(2E(2\delta))$.  Next,
$B=PQ$ with $Q\in\mathbb Z[x]$ since $P$ is monic, so $B(0)=P(0)Q(0)$ is a
nonzero multiple of $P(0)=\prod_j\rho_j^2\ge R^{2K}$, and $B(0)$ is a
coefficient of $F$; this gives $\Lambda(F)\ge2K\log R$.  Finally, for
$0<\delta\le\pi/4$ we have $1+\pi/(4\delta)\le\pi/(2\delta)$, so
$E(2\delta)\le(8\delta/\log2)(1+\log(\pi/(2\delta)))
=(8\delta/\log2)\log(e\pi/(2\delta))$.
\end{proof}

For the register roots of Polynomial, $\varphi=\pi/2$ and $\delta$ is the
largest angle $\arctan(|a_j|/c_j)$.

\begin{corollary}[Thin register clusters]\label{cor:thinregister}
Let $A\ge1$, $\lambda\ge1$ and $R\ge\max(6,A)$, and let
$\alpha_j=a_j+ic_j$ with $0<|a_j|\le A$ and $R\le c_j\le\lambda R$ for
$1\le j\le K$.  Put $\delta=\arctan(A/R)$ and let $F\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be a
nonzero multiple of $P$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item If $(K-2)\,\delta\log(e\pi/(2\delta))\le\frac{\pi\log2}2$, for instance
if $(K-2)\frac AR\log\frac{e\pi R}{2A}\le1.08$, then
\[
  \Lambda(F)\ \ge\ \frac{K(K-2)}8\log\Bigl(\frac R2-1\Bigr)
  \quad\text{and}\quad
  \Lambda(F)\ \ge\ \frac15\sum_{j=1}^Kj\log\rho_{(j)}-\frac{K(K+1)}{10}\log(3\sqrt2\,\lambda),
\]
where $\rho_{(1)}\le\cdots\le\rho_{(K)}$ are the $\rho_j$ in order.
\item If $K\le\kappa R/\log R$ for some $\kappa>0$, then
\[
  \Lambda(F)\ \ge\ \min\Bigl\{\frac{K(K-2)}8\log\Bigl(\frac R2-1\Bigr),\
  \frac{\pi\log2}{16}\cdot\frac{K^2\log R}{\kappa A}\cdot
  \frac{\log(R/2)}{\log(e\pi R/(2A))}\Bigr\}.
\]
If the $c_j$ are distinct primes, $\kappa=1.25506\,\lambda$ qualifies.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Every $\alpha_j$ has $|\alpha_j|\ge c_j\ge R$ and
$|\arg\alpha_j-\pi/2|=\arctan(|a_j|/c_j)\le\delta\le\pi/4$, so
Theorem~\ref{thm:thingauss} applies with $\varphi=\pi/2$.  The function
$x\log(e\pi/(2x))$ is increasing on $(0,\pi/2)$, with derivative
$\log(\pi/(2x))>0$, and $\delta\le A/R$; this gives the ``for instance'', as
$\frac{\pi\log2}2>1.0887$.

1.  For $K\le2$ the first bound is trivial, and the second follows from
$\Lambda(F)\ge2K\log R$ and the estimate for $\sum_jj\log\rho_{(j)}$ below,
since $K(K+1)/10\le2K$.  For $K\ge3$ the hypothesis and
the last inequality of Theorem~\ref{thm:thingauss} give
$\pi K\log(R/2)/(2E(2\delta))\ge\frac{K(K-2)}8\log(R/2)$, so the minimum there
is the first term.  For the second bound combine it with
$\Lambda(F)\ge2K\log R$ in the proportion $\frac45:\frac15$, and use
$\log(R/2-1)\ge\log R-\log3$ for $R\ge6$:
\[
  \Lambda(F)\ \ge\ \frac{K(K+2)}{10}\log R-\frac{K(K-2)}{10}\log3 .
\]
On the other side $\rho_j^2=a_j^2+c_j^2\le2\lambda^2R^2$, so
$\frac15\sum_jj\log\rho_{(j)}\le\frac{K(K+1)}{10}(\log R+\log(\sqrt2\lambda))$,
and the difference of the two displays is at least
$-\frac{K(K+1)}{10}\log(\sqrt2\lambda)-\frac{K(K-2)}{10}\log3
\ge-\frac{K(K+1)}{10}\log(3\sqrt2\lambda)$.

2.  By the last inequality of Theorem~\ref{thm:thingauss} and the
monotonicity above,
\[
  \frac{\pi K\log(R/2)}{2E(2\delta)}\ \ge\
  \frac{\pi\log2}{16}\cdot\frac{KR\log(R/2)}{A\log(e\pi R/(2A))},
\]
and $R\ge K\log R/\kappa$.  For distinct primes $c_j\le\lambda R$,
\[
  K\ \le\ \pi(\lambda R)\ <\ \frac{1.25506\,\lambda R}{\log(\lambda R)}
  \ \le\ \frac{1.25506\,\lambda R}{\log R}
\]
by~\cite{rosser-schoenfeld}.
\end{proof}

So for register roots whose operands are bounded, $|a_j|\le A$, the mass
bound of order $K^2\log R$ holds for $K$ roots in one annulus $[R,\lambda R]$
uniformly in $K$: with item~1 when $K\le cR/(A\log R)$, and, for prime
imaginary parts, with item~2 at any density, where
$\log(R/2)/\log(e\pi R/(2A))\ge0.33$ for $R\ge6$ and tends to $1$, so the
coefficient of $K^2\log R$ in the second term is at least $0.036/(\lambda A)$
and tends to $0.108/(\lambda A)$ as $R\to\infty$.  Applied to the roots of modulus at
least $s$, for each $s$, the theorem gives the largest of these bounds over
all tails, each with the angle $\delta_s=\max\{\arctan(|a_j|/c_j):|\alpha_j|\ge s\}$,
which for register roots decreases as $s$ grows.  The constants are far from
the truth at small $K$: at the four sets of Proposition~\ref{prop:gaussinst}
the theorem gives $0$, $0.15$, $1.45$ and $6.22$, below the constant-term bound
$2K\log R$, against the certified $11.09$, $26.20$, $58.22$ and $139.8$; the
certificates remain the way to settle individual root sets, and the theorem is
the uniform statement in $K$.

For real multiples the two crossing polynomials of
Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorcount} at the rays $\arg z=\frac\pi2\pm\delta'$ are one
real polynomial with zeros of both signs, and the crossings can be localized
annulus by annulus.

\begin{lemma}[Both rays, annulus by annulus]\label{lem:localcross}
Let $B\in\R[x]$ be nonzero of degree $d$, let $0<\delta'\le\pi/2$, and suppose
$B$ has no zero other than $0$ on the rays $\arg z=\frac\pi2\pm\delta'$.  Let
$0<A_1<\cdots<A_{m+1}$ with $A_{\ell+1}\ge2A_\ell$.  There are
$a_\ell\in[A_\ell/2,A_\ell)$, $1\le\ell\le m+1$, with the following property.
Put $\psi=\frac\pi2-\delta'$ and, for $\theta\in\R$,
\[
  h_\theta(r)=\operatorname{Im}\bigl(e^{-i\theta}B(re^{i\psi})\bigr)
  =\sum_{k=0}^d\operatorname{Im}\bigl(e^{-i\theta}b_ke^{ik\psi}\bigr)r^k .
\]
If $B$ has $K_\ell$ zeros, counted with multiplicity, in
$W_\ell=\{a_\ell<|z|<a_{\ell+1},\ |\arg z-\frac\pi2|<\delta'\}$, then for every
$\theta$ the real polynomial $h_\theta$ has at least
$2K_\ell-2dE(\delta')/\pi-2$ distinct real zeros $r$ with
$a_\ell<|r|<a_{\ell+1}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
With $S(r)$ as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorcount},
$\int_{A/2}^{A}S(r)\,dr/r\le dE(\delta')\log2$ for every $A>0$, so we may choose
$a_\ell\in[A_\ell/2,A_\ell)$ with $S(a_\ell)\le dE(\delta')$ and $a_\ell$
different from the modulus of every zero of $B$.  The intervals
$[A_\ell/2,A_\ell)$ are disjoint and increasing, so $a_1<\cdots<a_{m+1}$.  Let
$\psi_\pm=\frac\pi2\pm\delta'$ and let $\Delta_\pm^\ell$ be the change of a
continuous argument of $B(re^{i\psi_\pm})$ as $r$ increases from $a_\ell$ to
$a_{\ell+1}$.  The argument principle along $\partial W_\ell$ and the arc
bound $|\Delta_{\rm arc}(a)|\le S(a)$ give
\[
  2\pi K_\ell\ \le\ |\Delta^\ell_-|+|\Delta^\ell_+|+2dE(\delta').
\]
By the last paragraph of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorcount}, for every
$\theta'$ there are at least $|\Delta^\ell_\pm|/\pi-1$ distinct
$r\in(a_\ell,a_{\ell+1})$ at which $e^{-i\theta'}B(re^{i\psi_\pm})$ is real.  With
$\psi_-=\psi$ and $\theta'=\theta$ these are positive zeros of $h_\theta$.
For $r<0$ we have $re^{i\psi}=\overline{|r|e^{i\psi_+}}$, and $B$ is real, so
$h_\theta(r)=-\operatorname{Im}\bigl(e^{i\theta}B(|r|e^{i\psi_+})\bigr)$; with
$\psi_+$ and $\theta'=-\theta$ this gives at least $|\Delta^\ell_+|/\pi-1$
negative zeros with $a_\ell<|r|<a_{\ell+1}$.  Add the two counts.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Thin sectors at the imaginary axis]\label{thm:thinreal}
Let $B\in\R[x]$ have degree $d$ and $|b_d|\ge1$, let $0<\delta\le\pi/4$ and
$R\ge8$, and suppose that $B$ has at least $K$ zeros, counted with
multiplicity, in $\{z:|z|\ge R,\ |\arg z-\frac\pi2|\le\delta\}$.  Let $M$ be the
least integer with $M\ge2K-2dE(2\delta)/\pi-2$, and suppose $M\ge1$.  Then
$b_k(B)\ge|b_d|\bigl((R/4)^{M-2k+2}-1\bigr)/(2^k-1)$ for
$1\le k\le\lceil M/2\rceil$, and
\[
  \Lambda(B)\ \ge\ \Bigl\lfloor\frac{(M+1)^2}4\Bigr\rfloor\log\frac R4
  -\frac12\Bigl\lceil\frac M2\Bigr\rceil\Bigl(\Bigl\lceil\frac M2\Bigr\rceil+3\Bigr)\log2 .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Choose $\delta'\in(\delta,2\delta]$ with no zero of $B$ on the rays
$\arg z=\frac\pi2\pm\delta'$; then $E(\delta')\le E(2\delta)$ and the $K$ zeros
lie in the open sector.  Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:localcross} with $m=1$, $A_1=R$
and $A_2$ at least twice the largest modulus of a zero of $B$: then
$a_1\ge R/2$, $a_2$ exceeds every modulus, and $W_1$ contains the $K$ zeros.
So every $h_\theta$ has at least $M$ distinct real zeros, all of modulus
greater than $R/2\ge4$.  Choose $\theta$ with
$e^{-i\theta}b_de^{id\psi}=i|b_d|$; then $h_\theta$ is real of degree $d$ with
leading coefficient $|b_d|$ and coefficients
$|h_k|\le|b_k|$.  Corollary~\ref{cor:bothsignsmass}, with $R/2$ in place of
$R$, applies to $h_\theta$; the domination, with the leading coefficients at
the same position, gives $b_k(B)\ge b_k(h_\theta)$ and
$\Lambda(B)\ge\Lambda(h_\theta)$, and $\log|b_d|\ge0$.
\end{proof}

Under the hypothesis $dE(2\delta)\le\pi K/2$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:thinsector}
we get $M\ge K-2$ and a bound of about $\frac14K^2\log R$, twice the
$\frac18K^2\log R$ there.  When $dE(2\delta)\le\varepsilon\pi K$ we get
$M\ge2(1-\varepsilon)K-2$ and a bound $(1-\varepsilon)^2K^2\log R-O(K^2)$.  This
leading term is attained: the $K$ purely imaginary pairs $\pm ic_j$ with
$c_j\in[R,2R]$ lie in every such sector, and
$P_{\rm im}=\prod_j(x^2+c_j^2)$ has $\Lambda(P_{\rm im})\le K(K+1)\log(2R)+K^2\log2$
by the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:bothsignssharp}.  So for real multiples
and thin sectors at the imaginary axis the constant of
Corollary~\ref{cor:imag}, one real root $c_j^2$ per pair, is recovered up to
$O(K^2)$.

\begin{corollary}[Thin Gaussian clusters, both rays]\label{cor:thingaussreal}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:thingauss} with $\varphi=\pi/2$, let
$R\ge8$ and $0<\varepsilon<1$, and put
$M_\varepsilon=\lceil2(1-\varepsilon)K\rceil-2$; suppose $M_\varepsilon\ge1$.  Every
nonzero $F\in\mathbb Z[x]$ divisible by $P$ satisfies
\[
  \Lambda(F)\ \ge\ \min\Bigl\{
  \Bigl\lfloor\frac{(M_\varepsilon+1)^2}4\Bigr\rfloor\log\frac R4
  -\frac12\Bigl\lceil\frac{M_\varepsilon}2\Bigr\rceil
   \Bigl(\Bigl\lceil\frac{M_\varepsilon}2\Bigr\rceil+3\Bigr)\log2,\
  \frac{\varepsilon\pi K\log(R/2)}{E(2\delta)}\Bigr\}.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Let $B$ be a block of $F$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussblocks}), of degree $d$: it is
real, $|b_d|\ge1$, it vanishes at the $K$ distinct $\alpha_j$, and
$\Lambda(F)\ge\Lambda(B)$.  If $dE(2\delta)\le\varepsilon\pi K$ then
$2K-2dE(2\delta)/\pi-2\ge2(1-\varepsilon)K-2$, so the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:thinreal} gives a real polynomial $h_\theta$, dominated by $B$
with leading coefficient $|b_d|$ at the same position, with at least
$M_\varepsilon$ real zeros of modulus greater than $R/2$; Corollary~\ref{cor:bothsignsmass},
with $R/2$ in place of $R$, applied to $M_\varepsilon$ of them gives the first
term.  Otherwise $d>\varepsilon\pi K/E(2\delta)$, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge} with $\rho_{\min}\ge R>2$ gives
$\Lambda(B)\ge d\log(\rho_{\min}/2)$, which exceeds the second term.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{What remains open.}  Two cases of the register roots
$a_j\pm ic_j$, $0<|a_j|<c_j$, whose moduli crowd.

\emph{Wide sectors.}  Suppose the angles of $K$ roots of one annulus from
the imaginary axis, $\arctan(|a_j|/c_j)$, are not $O(1/(K\log K))$, for
instance $|a_j|$ comparable to $c_j$.  Then
Theorem~\ref{thm:thingauss} gives only a linear charge.  This is where the angle
mechanism of Proposition~\ref{prop:noangle} lives: $K$ roots spread over an
angle $\delta\gg1/K$ need degree only about $K/\delta$, which is
$o(K^2)$, and Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge} charges degree only linearly.  Exactly equal
moduli are excluded when the common norm is a prime power
(Corollary~\ref{cor:padiccircle}), but not for squarefree norms, and not for
nearly equal moduli; pairwise coprime norms get only the first row from
Theorem~\ref{thm:padicmass}, and inside one annulus
Theorem~\ref{thm:archnewton} gives no more.  Carry sizes, even at several
roots, cannot close the gap (Proposition~\ref{prop:carrylimit}); what is needed
is a lower bound for the residuals of the rounding recurrence of
Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussround} and its analogues with $k-1$ free coefficients.
Section~\ref{sec:widesectors} below makes the angle mechanism exact: every
multiple of degree $O(K)$ pays $(\pi/\delta-o(1))K\log R$, which
$x^N-\rho^N$ attains, and a bound of order $K^2\log R$ at Gaussian roots in a
sector of fixed width reduces to excluding integer multiples in which the
sector holds at most about its equidistributed share of the large zeros
(Corollary~\ref{cor:widedisc}).  Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction}
gives a second sufficient condition, which is purely Diophantine: the sector
contains few Gaussian integers that are zeros, to high relative precision,
of an integer polynomial with few terms; it has to be restricted to degrees
$O(s^2)$ and to sectors inside an open half-plane
(Propositions~\ref{prop:Hfails} and~\ref{prop:skeletoncap}).

\emph{Across annuli.}  Theorem~\ref{thm:thinreal}, applied to the roots of
modulus at least $s$ for each $s$, charges each thin tail with the sharp
constant, $K_s^2\log s$ for the $K_s$ roots of modulus at least $s$, but not the
cross terms $K_sK_t\log t$ ($s<t$) of $\sum_jj\log\rho_j^2$.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:localcross} the crossings are localized: with intermediate arcs
at radii $a_\ell$, the real polynomial $h_\theta$ has at least
$2K_\ell-2dE(\delta')/\pi-2$ zeros of both signs in each annulus
$a_\ell<|r|<a_{\ell+1}$.  What is missing is a mass bound for real multiples
with prescribed real roots of both signs that charges the larger modulus of a
pair of roots, as Theorem~\ref{thm:order} does for one sign, and not the smaller
one, as \eqref{eq:bothrowsbound} does.  We conjecture that for every nonzero
real multiple $F$ of $\prod_{i=1}^L(x-r_i)$ with $r_i$ real, $|r_i|\ge2$,
repetitions allowed, and moduli $\rho_1\le\cdots\le\rho_L$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bothrows}
  b_k(F)\ \ge\ |f_D|\prod_{i=2k-1}^{L}(\rho_i-1)\qquad
  \bigl(1\le k\le\lceil L/2\rceil\bigr).
\end{equation}
The row $k=1$ is Proposition~\ref{prop:rowone}, and the row $k=2$ holds up to the factor $13$, and with the factor $1$ when
the two roots of smallest modulus have opposite signs
(Theorem~\ref{thm:secondrowsharp}); for roots of one sign
\eqref{eq:bothrows} is weaker than Theorem~\ref{thm:order}; for pairs
$\pm s_j$ it follows from the multisection transfer, since
$s^2-1\ge(s-1)^2$; and it is what \eqref{eq:bothrowsbound} gives, up to the
factors $\rho$ and $(1-\rho)^{-k}$, when all moduli are equal.  Up to a factor
$(2\kappa)^{2k-2}$ in the $k$-th row, \eqref{eq:bothrows} follows from the
bound \eqref{eq:nuconj} on certificates with $2k-2$ spare roots, whose
qualitative form is Proposition~\ref{prop:sparedescartes}.  In exact
rational arithmetic, for random root sets with $3\le L\le6$, near-pairs
$r,-r(1+\eta)$ included, and random excluded sets, one of the three zero sets
$W\cup\{\text{first free distances}\}$, $W\cup(W-1)\cup\{\ldots\}$ and
$W\cup(W+1)\cup\{\ldots\}$ always gave a certificate proving the row
(in 50-digit arithmetic some came within a factor $1+10^{-9}$ of
\eqref{eq:bothrows}, at a near-pair with one larger root); the filler rule of
Lemma~\ref{lem:slack} alone can fail by a factor larger than $50$.  So the free
zeros must be placed according to the signs, and the tail bound of
Theorem~\ref{thm:tail} has no direct analogue for nodes of both signs.  The
route through $F(x)F(-x)=G(x^2)$ fails for a different reason: $G$ has the
roots $r_i^2$ of one sign, but its coefficients are quadratic in those of $F$,
and $\Lambda(G)$ is not bounded by $C\Lambda(F)+C'D$ (for $F=M\sum_{j\in S}x^j$
with $S\subset[0,|S|^2]$ a Sidon set, $\Lambda(F)=|S|\log M$ while
$\Lambda(G)\ge\binom{|S|}2\log M$).

\begin{corollary}[Conditional on \eqref{eq:bothrows}]\label{cor:conjmass}
If \eqref{eq:bothrows} holds, then every $F$ as there with $|f_D|\ge1$ has
\[
  \Lambda(F)\ \ge\ \Bigl(\Bigl\lceil\frac L2\Bigr\rceil+1\Bigr)\log|f_D|
  +\sum_{i=1}^L\Bigl\lceil\frac i2\Bigr\rceil\log(\rho_i-1)
  \ \ge\ \frac12\sum_{i=1}^Li\log(\rho_i-1),
\]
and the weights $\lceil i/2\rceil$ cannot be improved: for
$P=\prod_{j\le m}(x^2-s_j^2)$ with $s_1\le\cdots\le s_m$,
\[
  \sum_{j=1}^m2j\log s_j\ \le\ \Lambda(P)\ \le\ m^2\log2+\sum_{j=1}^m2j\log s_j,
  \qquad
  \sum_{i=1}^{2m}\Bigl\lceil\frac i2\Bigr\rceil\log(\rho_i-1)=\sum_{j=1}^m2j\log(s_j-1).
\]
In particular no bound $\Lambda(F)\ge c\sum_ii\log(\rho_i-1)-O(L)$ with an
absolute $c>\frac12$ holds: at $s_j=s\to\infty$ the ratio of the two sides for
$P$ is $(m+1)/(2m+1)+o(1)$, which tends to $\frac12$ as $m\to\infty$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
As in Corollary~\ref{cor:mass}: every product in \eqref{eq:bothrows} is at
least $1$, the positions are distinct, and $\log(\rho_i-1)$ occurs in the rows
$k$ with $2k-1\le i$, that is $\lceil i/2\rceil$ times.  For $P$, the sorted
moduli are $\rho_{2j-1}=\rho_{2j}=s_j$, and
$\prod_{i>m-j}s_i^2\le e_j(s_1^2,\ldots,s_m^2)\le\binom mj\prod_{i>m-j}s_i^2$;
summing the logarithms over $j$ counts $\log s_i^2$ exactly $i$ times.  At
$s_j=s$, $\sum_ii\log(\rho_i-1)=m(2m+1)\log(s-1)$ and
$\Lambda(P)=m(m+1)\log s+O(m^2)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Cross terms, conditionally]\label{cor:conjcross}
Let $B$, $\delta'$, $A_\ell$, $a_\ell$ and $K_\ell$ be as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:localcross}, with $|b_d|\ge1$ and $A_1\ge6$, and put
$M_\ell=\max\bigl(0,\lceil2K_\ell-2dE(\delta')/\pi-2\rceil\bigr)$ and
$M_{<\ell}=\sum_{\ell'<\ell}M_{\ell'}$.  If \eqref{eq:bothrows} holds, then
\[
  \Lambda(B)\ \ge\ \sum_{\ell=1}^m\sum_{j=1}^{M_\ell}
  \Bigl\lceil\frac{M_{<\ell}+j}2\Bigr\rceil\log(a_\ell-1)
  \ \ge\ \sum_{\ell=1}^m\Bigl(\frac{M_\ell M_{<\ell}}2+\frac{M_\ell^2}4\Bigr)
  \log\Bigl(\frac{A_\ell}2-1\Bigr).
\]
In particular, if $2dE(\delta')/\pi+2\le2\varepsilon K_\ell$ for every $\ell$,
then
$\Lambda(B)\ge(1-\varepsilon)^2\sum_\ell(2K_\ell K_{<\ell}+K_\ell^2)\log(A_\ell/2-1)$,
which is $(1-\varepsilon)^2\sum_j(2j-1)\log(A_{\ell(j)}/2-1)$ over the $K=\sum_\ell K_\ell$
roots ordered by annulus, $\ell(j)$ being the annulus of the $j$-th: the
imaginary-pair charge $\sum_jj\log\rho_j^2$ of Corollary~\ref{cor:imag}, cross
terms included, up to replacing each modulus by the inner radius of its
annulus.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Choose $\theta$ with $e^{-i\theta}b_de^{id\psi}=i|b_d|$, so that $h=h_\theta$ is
real of degree $d$, has leading coefficient $|b_d|$, and is dominated by $B$.
By Lemma~\ref{lem:localcross} it has at least $M_\ell$ real zeros with
$a_\ell<|r|<a_{\ell+1}$; keep exactly $M_\ell$ of them in each annulus.  Sorted
by modulus, the $i$-th kept zero with $M_{<\ell}<i\le M_{<\ell}+M_\ell$ has
modulus greater than $a_\ell\ge A_\ell/2\ge3$.  Corollary~\ref{cor:conjmass}
gives $\Lambda(h)\ge\sum_i\lceil i/2\rceil\log(\rho_i-1)$, which is at least the
first sum, and $\Lambda(B)\ge\Lambda(h)$.  The second inequality uses
$\lceil x/2\rceil\ge x/2$.  For the last statement, $M_\ell\ge2(1-\varepsilon)K_\ell$.
\end{proof}

Both corollaries need \eqref{eq:bothrows} only up to a factor $C^{k-1}$
in the $k$-th row, with an absolute constant $C$; \eqref{eq:nuconj} would give
$C=4\kappa^2$.  Summing the rows as in the proof of
Corollary~\ref{cor:conjmass}, such a factor costs
$\frac12\lceil L/2\rceil(\lceil L/2\rceil-1)\log C=O(L^2)$ there, and
$O\bigl((\sum_\ell M_\ell)^2\bigr)$ in Corollary~\ref{cor:conjcross}; this is
of lower order than the main terms as soon as the moduli, respectively the
radii $A_\ell$, tend to infinity.  A factor $C^L$ in every row costs at most
$\lceil L/2\rceil L\log C$, which is again $O(L^2)$, so for these applications
\eqref{eq:nuconj} may be weakened to
$N_{2k-2}(y;W)\le C^L\prod_{j\le2k-2}\rho_j$.  The number of spare roots is
less flexible.  With $2k-2+a$ spare roots in the $k$-th row, $a\ge0$ fixed,
the factor $\log(\rho_i-1)$ is counted at least $\lceil i/2\rceil-\lceil a/2\rceil$ times.
The loss is at most $\lceil a/2\rceil\sum_i\log(\rho_i-1)$.  By Chebyshev's sum
inequality this is at most $4\lceil a/2\rceil/L$ times the main term of
Corollary~\ref{cor:conjmass}.  With $\alpha k$ spare roots, $\alpha>2$, it is
counted only about $i/\alpha$ times, and a fixed fraction $1-2/\alpha$ of the
main term is lost.

For Gaussian roots in $\mathbb Z[i]$ and $F\in\mathbb Z[x]$ the degree
hypothesis is removed as in Corollary~\ref{cor:thingaussreal}: a block of
degree $d$ with $2dE(\delta')/\pi+2>2\varepsilon\min_\ell K_\ell$ pays
$d\log(\rho_{\min}/2)$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge}.  So
\eqref{eq:bothrows} would complete the thin case at every scale, cross terms
included; unconditionally we have the thin tails of Theorem~\ref{thm:thinreal}
and, from Theorem~\ref{thm:order} applied to the positive zeros of $h_\theta$
alone, the cross terms among the crossings of one ray.

Perturbing the certificate of Corollary~\ref{cor:imag} to the nodes
$1/\alpha_j$ did not help either: the excluded coefficients are unbounded, so
the certificate must vanish there exactly, and the correction is controlled
only through certificate coefficients as large as an inverse Vandermonde
determinant in the close nodes $c_j^{-2}$.  Integral certificates (vectors of
the dual of the lattice of multiples) replace the normalization but not the
exact vanishing, and by transference they are as hard to find as the
bound itself.  Proposition~\ref{prop:gaussinst} settles individual root sets;
the question is a uniform statement in $K$ for crowded moduli at wide
angles, and the cross terms between annuli.

\subsection{Sectors of every width}\label{sec:widesectors}

The loss $dE(\delta')/\pi$ in Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorcount} is what confines
Theorems~\ref{thm:thinsector} and~\ref{thm:thinreal} to thin sectors.  This
subsection replaces it by $\delta'n/\pi$, where $n$ counts only the zeros of
modulus above the inner arc, plus a degree term that becomes small when the
inner radius is averaged over a long range of scales.  The rate
$\delta'/\pi$ is the share of a sector of opening $2\delta'$ in a full turn,
so the crossings measure how far the large zeros crowd into the sector beyond
their equidistributed share, and $x^N-\rho^N$ shows that no smaller rate is
possible.  The outcome describes, up to lower-order terms, what the angle
mechanism of Proposition~\ref{prop:noangle} costs at every width.  $K$ zeros of modulus at least $R$ in a
sector of half-width $\delta<\pi/2$ cost either about $\frac12m^2\log R$,
where $m$ is the excess of $K$ over the share $\delta n/\pi$, or about
$n\log R$ through the Mahler measure.  So every multiple of degree $O(K)$
pays $(\pi/\delta-o(1))K\log R$, and this constant is attained
(Theorem~\ref{thm:widesector}, Corollary~\ref{cor:wideconst},
Proposition~\ref{prop:widesharp}).  At Gaussian roots the degree is paid for
by Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge}, which makes the bound unconditional
(Corollary~\ref{cor:widegauss}).  In wide sectors the analogue
$\Lambda\ge cK^2\log R$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:thinreal} is therefore false for
real multiples, the correct order being $\min(K^2,K/\delta)\log R$, and at
Gaussian roots it reduces to excluding integer multiples in which the sector
holds at most about its equidistributed share of the large zeros
(Corollary~\ref{cor:widedisc}).

For $0<\delta\le\pi/2$ put
\[
  \kappa(\delta)=\frac1\pi\int_0^1\min\Bigl(2\arcsin q,\ \frac{2\delta q}{1-q}\Bigr)\frac{dq}q .
\]
The integrand increases with $\delta$ and is at most $2\arcsin(q)/q$, so
$\kappa$ is increasing and, by dominated convergence, continuous.  Since
$\int_0^1\arcsin(q)\,dq/q=\int_0^{\pi/2}s\cot s\,ds=-\int_0^{\pi/2}\log\sin s\,ds
=\frac\pi2\log2$, we have $\kappa(\delta)\le\log2$; and bounding
$2\arcsin q\le\pi q$ and splitting at $q=1-2\delta/\pi$,
\[
  \kappa(\delta)\ \le\ \frac1\pi\Bigl(\int_0^{1-2\delta/\pi}\frac{2\delta\,dq}{1-q}
  +\int_{1-2\delta/\pi}^1\pi\,dq\Bigr)
  =\frac{2\delta}\pi\Bigl(1+\log\frac\pi{2\delta}\Bigr).
\]

\begin{lemma}[Sharp crossings next to a sector]\label{lem:sharpcross}
Let $B\in\mathbb C[x]$ be nonzero of degree $d$, let $\varphi\in\R$ and
$0<\delta'\le\pi/2$, and suppose that $B$ has no zero other than $0$ on the
rays $\arg z=\varphi\pm\delta'$.  Let $0<a<b$ and
$\varepsilon=\kappa(\delta')d/\log(b/a)$.  There is $r_1\in[a,b)$, different
from the modulus of every zero of $B$, with the following property.  Let $n$
be the number of zeros of $B$ of modulus greater than $r_1$, and $N$ the
number of those in the open sector $|\arg z-\varphi|<\delta'$, both counted
with multiplicity.  For $\psi\in\R$ and $\theta\in\R$ put
$h_\theta(r)=\operatorname{Im}\bigl(e^{-i\theta}B(re^{i\psi})\bigr)$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item There is $\psi\in\{\varphi-\delta',\varphi+\delta'\}$ such that for
every $\theta$ the polynomial $h_\theta$ has at least
$N-\delta'n/\pi-\varepsilon-1$ distinct zeros in $(r_1,\infty)$.
\item If $B\in\R[x]$, $\varphi=\frac\pi2$ and $\psi=\frac\pi2-\delta'$, then
for every $\theta$ the polynomial $h_\theta$ has at least
$2N-2\delta'n/\pi-2\varepsilon-2$ distinct real zeros $r$ with $|r|>r_1$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For $r>0$ let $\Gamma_r$ and $\Delta_\beta(r)$ be as in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorcount}, let $[\,\cdot\,]$ be the indicator of a
condition, and for $\beta\ne0$ with $|\beta|\ne r$ put
$q=\min(|\beta|/r,r/|\beta|)<1$.  We claim
\[
  \bigl|\Delta_\beta(r)-2\delta'[\,|\beta|<r\,]\bigr|\ \le\
  c(q)=\min\Bigl(2\arcsin q,\ \frac{2\delta'q}{1-q}\Bigr).
\]
If $|\beta|<r$, write $re^{it}-\beta=re^{it}(1-w)$ with
$w=(\beta/r)e^{-it}$; if $|\beta|>r$, write $re^{it}-\beta=-\beta(1-w)$ with
$w=(r/\beta)e^{it}$.  In both cases $|w|=q<1$, so $1-w$ stays in the disc of
radius $q$ about $1$, where the principal argument is continuous and at most
$\arcsin q$ in absolute value.  So $\Delta_\beta(r)-2\delta'[\,|\beta|<r\,]$
is the change of $\operatorname{Arg}(1-w)$ along $\Gamma_r$, which is at most
$2\arcsin q$ in absolute value; and since $dw/dt=\mp iw$, the derivative
$\frac{d}{dt}\operatorname{Arg}(1-w)=\operatorname{Im}\frac{\pm iw}{1-w}$ is at most $q/(1-q)$ in
absolute value, over a range of length $2\delta'$.  For $\beta=0$,
$\Delta_0(r)=2\delta'$ exactly.

Let $\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_d$ be the zeros of $B$ with multiplicity (all but
the zero at $0$ are in the sum below) and
$S(r)=\sum_{\beta_l\ne0}c(q_l(r))$.  For each $\beta_l\ne0$,
\[
  \int_{-\infty}^{\infty}c\bigl(q_l(e^u)\bigr)\,du
  =2\int_0^\infty c(e^{-v})\,dv=2\int_0^1c(q)\frac{dq}q=2\pi\kappa(\delta'),
\]
so $\int_{\log a}^{\log b}S(e^u)\,du\le2\pi\kappa(\delta')d$ and
$S(e^u)\le2\pi\varepsilon$ on a subset of $[\log a,\log b)$ of positive
measure.  Choose $r_1=e^u$ there, different from every $|\beta_l|$.

Put $X_1=N-\delta'n/\pi-\varepsilon-1$ and $X_2=2X_1$, and choose $\eta>0$
with $\lceil X_1-\eta\rceil=\lceil X_1\rceil$ and
$\lceil X_2-2\eta\rceil=\lceil X_2\rceil$.  Since $c(q)\le\pi q$, $S(r)\to0$ as
$r\to\infty$; choose $r_2$ larger than $r_1$ and every $|\beta_l|$ with
$S(r_2)\le2\pi\eta$.  Let $\psi_\pm=\varphi\pm\delta'$ and let $\Delta_\pm$ be
the change of a continuous argument of $B(re^{i\psi_\pm})$ as $r$ increases
from $r_1$ to $r_2$.  The region
$W=\{r_1<|z|<r_2,\ |\arg z-\varphi|<\delta'\}$ has no zero of $B$ on its
boundary and contains exactly $N$ zeros.  With $\Delta_{\rm arc}(r)$ as in
the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorcount}, the argument principle gives
$2\pi N=\Delta_-+\Delta_{\rm arc}(r_2)-\Delta_+-\Delta_{\rm arc}(r_1)$.  By
the claim,
$\Delta_{\rm arc}(r)=\sum_l\Delta_{\beta_l}(r)=2\delta'\#\{l:|\beta_l|<r\}+\epsilon(r)$
with $|\epsilon(r)|\le S(r)$, and
$\#\{l:|\beta_l|<r_2\}-\#\{l:|\beta_l|<r_1\}=n$.  Hence
\[
  |\Delta_-|+|\Delta_+|\ \ge\ |\Delta_--\Delta_+|
  \ \ge\ 2\pi N-2\delta'n-S(r_1)-S(r_2)
  \ \ge\ 2\pi N-2\delta'n-2\pi\varepsilon-2\pi\eta .
\]
By the last paragraph of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorcount}, for every
$\theta'$ there are at least $|\Delta_\pm|/\pi-1$ distinct
$r\in(r_1,r_2)$ at which $e^{-i\theta'}B(re^{i\psi_\pm})$ is real.

1.  Let $\psi$ be the ray with the larger $|\Delta_\pm|$.  Then
$|\Delta_\psi|/\pi-1\ge X_1-\eta$, so every $h_\theta$ has at least
$\lceil X_1-\eta\rceil=\lceil X_1\rceil\ge X_1$ distinct zeros in
$(r_1,\infty)$.

2.  As in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:localcross}, the ray $\psi_-=\psi$ with
$\theta'=\theta$ gives positive zeros of $h_\theta$, and for $r<0$,
$re^{i\psi}=\overline{|r|e^{i\psi_+}}$ gives
$h_\theta(r)=-\operatorname{Im}\bigl(e^{i\theta}B(|r|e^{i\psi_+})\bigr)$, so the
ray $\psi_+$ with $\theta'=-\theta$ gives negative zeros.  Together there are
at least $(|\Delta_-|+|\Delta_+|)/\pi-2\ge X_2-2\eta$ of them, hence at least
$\lceil X_2\rceil$.
\end{proof}

The loss is charged only to the $n$ zeros above the inner arc, at the
equidistribution rate $\delta'/\pi$; the degree enters only through
$\varepsilon$.  With $b/a=2$, as in Lemma~\ref{lem:sectorcount}, the total
loss is at most
$d\bigl(\delta'/\pi+2\delta'(1+\log\frac\pi{2\delta'})/(\pi\log2)\bigr)$,
already about half of $dE(\delta')/\pi$; with $\log(b/a)$ large it tends to
$\delta'n/\pi$.

\begin{theorem}[Sectors of every width]\label{thm:widesector}
Let $B\in\mathbb C[x]$ have degree $d$ and $|b_d|\ge1$, let $\varphi\in\R$,
$0<\delta<\pi/2$, $T>1$ and $R\ge3T$, and suppose that $B$ has at least $K$
zeros, counted with multiplicity, in
$\{z:|z|\ge R,\ |\arg z-\varphi|\le\delta\}$.  Put
$e=\kappa(\delta)d/\log T$ and, for $\nu\ge0$,
$g(\nu)=\nu\log(R/T)-\log(\nu+1)-1$.  For every integer $m\ge0$,
\[
  \Lambda(B)\ \ge\ \min\Bigl\{\frac{m(m+1)}2\log\Bigl(\frac RT-1\Bigr),\
  g(\nu_m)\Bigr\},\qquad
  \nu_m=\frac\pi\delta\,(K-1-m-e)^+ .
\]
If moreover $B\in\R[x]$, $\varphi=\frac\pi2$ and $R\ge4T$, then for every
integer $m\ge1$,
\[
  \Lambda(B)\ \ge\ \min\Bigl\{\Bigl\lfloor\frac{(m+1)^2}4\Bigr\rfloor\log\frac R{2T}
  -\frac12\Bigl\lceil\frac m2\Bigr\rceil\Bigl(\Bigl\lceil\frac m2\Bigr\rceil+3\Bigr)\log2,\
  g(\nu'_m)\Bigr\},
\]
where $\nu'_m=\frac\pi{2\delta}(2K-2-2e-m)^+$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Choose $\delta'\in(\delta,\pi/2)$ such that no zero of $B$ other than $0$ lies
on the rays $\arg z=\varphi\pm\delta'$; all but finitely many values qualify.
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:sharpcross} with $a=R/T$ and $b=R$, so
$\varepsilon'=\kappa(\delta')d/\log T$; the $K$ zeros have modulus at least
$R>r_1$ and lie in the open sector, so $N\ge K$.  Note $r_1\ge R/T\ge3$, and
$g$ is increasing on $[0,\infty)$, as $g'(\nu)=\log(R/T)-1/(\nu+1)>0$.

\emph{Mahler measure.}  Suppose $n\ge1$ and let
$B^*(t)=t^dB(1/t)=\sum_kb_{d-k}t^k$, so $B^*(0)=b_d$ and the nonzero zeros of
$B^*$ are the $1/\beta$, $\beta\ne0$ a zero of $B$.  Jensen's formula on
$|t|=\varrho=n/(n+1)\ge\frac12$, where the $n$ points $1/\beta$ with
$|\beta|>r_1$ lie in $|t|<1/3$ and each contributes $\log(\varrho|\beta|)\ge\log(\varrho r_1)$,
and every other contribution is nonnegative, gives
\[
  \log\max_{|t|=\varrho}|B^*(t)|\ \ge\ \log|b_d|+n\log(\varrho r_1)\ \ge\ n\log r_1-1,
\]
using $n\log\frac{n+1}n\le1$.  On the other side
$\max_{|t|=\varrho}|B^*|\le\max_i|b_i|\sum_k\varrho^k=(n+1)\max_i|b_i|$.  So
$\Lambda(B)\ge\log\max_i|b_i|\ge n\log r_1-\log(n+1)-1\ge g(n)$.

\emph{Crossings.}  Choose $\theta$ with $e^{-i\theta}b_de^{id\psi}=i|b_d|$.  As
in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:thinsector}, $h=h_\theta$ is real of degree
$d$ with leading coefficient $|b_d|\ge1$ and $|h_k|\le|b_k|$, so
$\Lambda(B)\ge\Lambda(h)$.

For the first bound let $X_1=N-\delta'n/\pi-\varepsilon'-1$.  If $X_1\ge m$, then by
item~1 of Lemma~\ref{lem:sharpcross} $h$ has $m$ distinct zeros larger than
$r_1\ge3$, and Theorem~\ref{thm:order} with Corollary~\ref{cor:mass} gives
$\Lambda(h)\ge\sum_{i=1}^mi\log(\varrho_i-1)\ge\frac{m(m+1)}2\log(R/T-1)$.
Otherwise $n>(\pi/\delta')(K-1-m-\varepsilon')$.  If the right side is positive,
$n\ge1$ and $\Lambda(B)\ge g(n)\ge g(\nu_m(\delta'))$, where $\nu_m(\delta')$
is $\nu_m$ with $\delta'$ and $\varepsilon'$ in place of $\delta$ and $e$; if not,
$\nu_m(\delta')=0$ and $g(0)=-1<0\le\Lambda(B)$.  So the first bound holds with
$\nu_m(\delta')$, for all admissible $\delta'$ arbitrarily close to $\delta$;
as $\delta'\downarrow\delta$, $\kappa(\delta')\to\kappa(\delta)$ and
$\nu_m(\delta')\to\nu_m$, and $g$ is continuous.

For the second bound let $X_2=2N-2\delta'n/\pi-2\varepsilon'-2$.  If $X_2\ge m$,
then by item~2 of Lemma~\ref{lem:sharpcross} $h$ has $m$ distinct real zeros of
modulus greater than $r_1\ge R/T\ge4$, and Corollary~\ref{cor:bothsignsmass},
with $R/T$ in place of $R$, applied to these $m$ zeros, with the terms in
$\log|b_d|\ge0$ dropped, gives the first term.  Otherwise
$n>(\pi/(2\delta'))(2K-2-2\varepsilon'-m)$, and we conclude as before.
\end{proof}

The first term is the thin-sector mechanism, now at every width; the second
is the angle mechanism.  If the zeros of $B$ of modulus above $R/T$ are few,
for instance if $B=PQ$ with $P$ the product of $K$ conjugate pairs in the
sector and all zeros of $Q$ in $|z|\le R/T$, then $n\le2K$ and the
theorem with the largest admissible $m$ gives
$\Lambda(B)\ge\frac12\bigl((1-\frac{2\delta}\pi)K-e-2\bigr)^2\log(R/T-1)$, and at
the imaginary axis about $(1-\frac{2\delta}\pi)^2K^2\log R$.  So in a sector of any
width short of a half-plane a cheap multiple needs about
$(\frac\pi\delta-2)K$ further zeros of modulus comparable to $R$, which the
Mahler term charges linearly.

\begin{corollary}[The constant $\pi/\delta$]\label{cor:wideconst}
In Theorem~\ref{thm:widesector} suppose $d\le\lambda K$ with $\lambda>0$, let
$0<\varepsilon\le\frac12$, and take $T=\exp(\kappa(\delta)\lambda/\varepsilon)$,
assuming $R\ge3T$.  Then
\[
  \Lambda(B)\ \ge\ \min\Bigl\{\frac{\varepsilon^2K^2}2\log\Bigl(\frac RT-1\Bigr),\
  \frac\pi\delta\bigl((1-2\varepsilon)K-2\bigr)\log\frac RT
  -\log\Bigl(\frac{\pi K}\delta+1\Bigr)-1\Bigr\}.
\]
Consequently, for fixed $\delta$ and $\lambda$,
$\Lambda(B)\ge(\pi/\delta-o(1))K\log R$ as $K\to\infty$ and $R\to\infty$.
If $B$ is real, $\varphi=\frac\pi2$ and $R\ge4T$, the same holds with the first
term replaced by $\varepsilon^2K^2\log(R/(2T))-\frac12(\varepsilon K+1)(\varepsilon K+4)\log2$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Here $e\le\kappa(\delta)\lambda K/\log T=\varepsilon K$.  Take
$m=\lceil\varepsilon K\rceil\le\varepsilon K+1$: then
$\frac{m(m+1)}2\ge\frac{\varepsilon^2K^2}2$ and
$K-1-m-e\ge(1-2\varepsilon)K-2=:\nu_0\delta/\pi$.  Since $g$ is increasing,
$g(\nu_m)\ge g(\nu_0^+)$, and $g(\nu_0)\ge\nu_0\log(R/T)-\log(\pi K/\delta+1)-1$
because $\nu_0\le\pi K/\delta$; if $\nu_0<0$ the claimed bound is negative.
For the limit take $\varepsilon=\max(K^{-1/3},(\log R)^{-1/2})$: then
$\log T=O((\log R)^{1/2})$, the first term is at least
$\frac12K^{4/3}\log(R/T-1)$, which exceeds the second for large $K$, and the second is
$(\pi/\delta)(1-o(1))K\log R$.  For the real case take $m=\lceil2\varepsilon K\rceil$
in the second bound of Theorem~\ref{thm:widesector}: then
$2K-2-2e-m\ge2((1-2\varepsilon)K-2)+1$, $\lfloor(m+1)^2/4\rfloor\ge\varepsilon^2K^2$ and
$\lceil m/2\rceil=\lceil\varepsilon K\rceil\le\varepsilon K+1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Sharpness of $\pi/\delta$]\label{prop:widesharp}
Let $\varphi\in\R$, $0<\delta\le\pi$, $K\ge1$, $\rho\ge1$ and
$N=\lceil\pi K/\delta\rceil$.  The monic polynomial $x^N-\rho^N$ has at least
$K$ zeros in $\{z:|z|=\rho,\ |\arg z-\varphi|\le\delta\}$, degree
$N\le\pi K/\delta+1$, and
\[
  \Lambda(x^N-\rho^N)=N\log\rho\ \le\ \Bigl(\frac{\pi K}\delta+1\Bigr)\log\rho .
\]
It is real, and integral when $\rho^N\in\mathbb Z$.  If $0<\varphi-\delta$
and $\varphi+\delta<\pi$, it is a real multiple of $K$ conjugate pairs
$\beta_j,\bar\beta_j$ with $\beta_j$ in the sector.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The zeros $\rho e^{2\pi il/N}$ have arguments spaced by $2\pi/N$, so a closed
arc of length $2\delta$ contains at least $\lfloor\delta N/\pi\rfloor\ge K$ of
them.  The only nonleading nonzero coefficient is $-\rho^N$.  Under the last
hypothesis the $K$ zeros are nonreal, and their conjugates are zeros of the
real polynomial $x^N-\rho^N$.
\end{proof}

Its degree is at most $(\pi/\delta+1)K$, so Corollary~\ref{cor:wideconst}
applies with $\lambda=\pi/\delta+1$: the constant $\pi/\delta$ of the angle
mechanism is sharp in every sector.  The thin-sector term is sharp too: on a
common ray, $\prod_j(x-\rho_je^{i\varphi})$ with $\rho_j\in[R,2R]$ has
$\Lambda\le\frac{K(K+1)}2\log(2R)+K^2\log2$, and at the imaginary axis
$\prod_j(x^2+c_j^2)$ matches the second bound of Theorem~\ref{thm:widesector}
(the remark after Theorem~\ref{thm:thinreal}).  So for complex multiples of
degree $O(K)$ the infimum of $\Lambda$ is of order $\min(K^2,K/\delta)\log R$
as $R\to\infty$, with both constants attained in the limit.  In a wide sector, with $\delta$
fixed, it is linear in $K$, and
$\Lambda\ge cK^2\log R$ fails already for the monic real multiple $x^N-\rho^N$.
For real multiples of conjugate pairs at an angle $\varphi\ne\frac\pi2$ the
constant $\frac12$ of the first term is not known to be sharp: both members of a
pair can be charged only at the imaginary axis, and the products give larger
constants, for instance $\frac32$ from $\prod_j(x^3+\rho_j^3)$ at
$\varphi=\frac\pi3$.

\begin{corollary}[Gaussian roots in sectors of every width]\label{cor:widegauss}
In the setting of Section~\ref{sec:gaussian}, let $\varphi\in\R$,
$0<\delta<\pi/2$, $T>1$, $R\ge4T$ and $\lambda>0$, suppose that every
$\alpha_j$ has $|\alpha_j|\ge R$ and $|\arg\alpha_j-\varphi|\le\delta$, and put
$e=\kappa(\delta)\lambda K/\log T$.  Every nonzero $F\in\mathbb Z[x]$ divisible
by $P$ satisfies, for every integer $m\ge0$,
\[
  \Lambda(F)\ \ge\ \min\Bigl\{\lambda K\log\frac R2,\
  \frac{m(m+1)}2\log\Bigl(\frac RT-1\Bigr),\ g(\nu_m)\Bigr\},
\]
with $g$ and $\nu_m$ as in Theorem~\ref{thm:widesector}, and, if
$\varphi=\frac\pi2$, the same with the second and third terms replaced by those
of the second bound there, for every integer $m\ge1$.  In particular, for fixed
$\delta$, $\Lambda(F)\ge(\pi/\delta-o(1))K\log R$ as $K\to\infty$ and
$R\to\infty$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Let $B$ be a block of $F$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussblocks}), of degree $d$; it is
real, $|b_d|\ge1$, it vanishes at the $K$ distinct $\alpha_j$, and
$\Lambda(F)\ge\Lambda(B)$.  If $d\ge\lambda K$, Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge}
with $\rho_{\min}\ge R>2$ gives $\Lambda(B)\ge\lambda K\log(R/2)$.  Otherwise
$\kappa(\delta)d/\log T\le e$, and Theorem~\ref{thm:widesector} applies to $B$;
its bounds only improve when $e$ decreases, since $\nu_m$ and $\nu'_m$ then
increase and $g$ is increasing.  For the last statement take
$\lambda=\pi/\delta$ and argue as in Corollary~\ref{cor:wideconst}.
\end{proof}

For thin sectors the choice $T=(e\pi/(2\delta))^{2/\varepsilon}$, which
makes $\kappa(\delta)\pi/(\delta\log T)\le\varepsilon$, turns the linear term
$\pi K\log(R/2)/(2E(2\delta))$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:thingauss} into
$(\pi/\delta)(1-O(\varepsilon))K\log R$ as $R\to\infty$, a gain of the factor $2E(2\delta)/\delta\ge23$ and of the
logarithm in $E$.  When $\delta$ is as small as a power of $1/R$, as for register roots,
$\log T$ is comparable to $\log R$, and Theorems~\ref{thm:thingauss}
and~\ref{thm:thinreal} with $T=2$ remain preferable.

The obstruction left at Gaussian roots is the equidistribution of large
zeros, and it can be isolated as an inequality of Erd\H{o}s--Tur\'an type.

\begin{corollary}[Crowding costs quadratically]\label{cor:widedisc}
Let $B\in\mathbb C[x]$ have degree $d$ and $|b_d|\ge1$, let $\varphi\in\R$,
$0<\delta<\pi/2$, $T>1$ and $R\ge3T$.  Let $K$ be the number of zeros of $B$
in $\{z:|z|\ge R,\ |\arg z-\varphi|\le\delta\}$ and $n_T$ the number of zeros
of modulus greater than $R/T$, both with multiplicity.  Then
\[
  K\ \le\ \frac\delta\pi\,n_T+\frac{\kappa(\delta)d}{\log T}+1
  +\sqrt{\frac{2\Lambda(B)}{\log(R/T-1)}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
With $\delta'$, $r_1$, $n$ and $X_1$ as in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:widesector}, $n\le n_T$ because $r_1\ge R/T$, so
$X_1\ge K-\delta'n_T/\pi-\varepsilon'-1$.  If $X_1>0$, then $h$ has
$m=\lceil X_1\rceil$ distinct zeros larger than $r_1$, and
$\Lambda(B)\ge\frac{m(m+1)}2\log(R/T-1)>\frac{X_1^2}2\log(R/T-1)$.  So
$X_1<\sqrt{2\Lambda(B)/\log(R/T-1)}$ in all cases; let $\delta'\downarrow\delta$.
\end{proof}

So a sector can hold more than its equidistributed share $\delta n_T/\pi$ of
the large zeros only at a mass quadratic in the excess, and, by the Mahler
term in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:widesector}, $n_T$ large zeros cost
$n_T\log(R/T)-O(\log n_T)$.  At Gaussian roots in a sector of fixed width,
apply the corollary to a block $B$ of $F$, of degree
$d\le\Lambda(F)/\log(R/2)$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge}: if
$\Lambda(F)=o(K^2\log R)$ and $\log T=o(\log R)$, then
$K\le(\delta/\pi)n_T+\kappa(\delta)d/\log T+o(K)$.  So a bound
$\Lambda(F)\ge cK^2\log R$ follows once integer multiples of $P$ are excluded
in which the sector holds at most about its equidistributed share of the large
zeros, up to the degree term.  Over $\R$ the polynomial $x^N-\rho^N$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:widesharp} is such a multiple, and every argument of
this subsection applies to it verbatim.  At Gaussian roots it is not available
off the axes and the diagonals.  If $\alpha\in\mathbb Z[i]$ and
$\alpha^N\in\R$, then $\alpha/\bar\alpha$ is a root of unity in
$\mathbb Q(i)$, so $\alpha^4\in\R$ and $\arg\alpha\in\frac\pi4\mathbb Z$.
Whether some less symmetric integer multiple does the same is the wide-sector
question, and it needs integrality beyond Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge}.

\paragraph{Sparse skeletons.}
Corollary~\ref{cor:widedisc} describes a cheap multiple by the angles of its
large zeros.  Its coefficients give a second, arithmetic description.  On
$|z|\ge R>1$ a polynomial of small mass agrees, up to a relative error
$|z|^{-t}$, with a polynomial that has few terms below its top $t+1$
degrees.  At Gaussian roots the error is measured in Gaussian integers.

\begin{lemma}[Sparse skeletons]\label{lem:skeleton}
Let $B=\sum_{k=0}^db_kx^k\in\mathbb C[x]$ have degree $d\ge1$ and
$|b_d|\ge1$, let $R>1$, and let $t\ge0$ be an integer.  Put
\[
  J=\{k:\ b_k\ne0,\ |b_k|\ge R^{d-t-k}\},\qquad
  S=\sum_{k\in J}b_kx^k,\qquad E=B-S .
\]
Then $J$ contains every $k\ge d-t$ with $b_k\ne0$, in particular $d$.  The
number $m$ of elements $k<d-t$ of $J$ satisfies
$m(m+1)/2\le\Lambda(B)/\log R$, so $S$ has at most $t+1+m$ nonzero
coefficients.  Moreover $|E(z)|<d\,|z|^{d-t}$ for every $z$ with
$|z|\ge R$, and $S,E\in\mathbb Z[x]$ if $B\in\mathbb Z[x]$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
If $k\ge d-t$ and $b_k\ne0$, then $|b_k|\ge1\ge R^{d-t-k}$, so $k\in J$.
If $k\in J$ and $k<d-t$, then $|b_k|\ge R^{d-t-k}>1$, so
$\log^+|b_k|\ge(d-t-k)\log R$.  The $m$ numbers $d-t-k$ are distinct
positive integers, so their sum is at least $m(m+1)/2$, while the
corresponding $\log^+|b_k|$ sum to at most $\Lambda(B)$.  Every nonzero
term $b_kz^k$ of $E$ has $k<d-t$ and $|b_k|<R^{d-t-k}$.  For $|z|\ge R$ this
gives $|b_kz^k|<R^{d-t-k}|z|^k\le|z|^{d-t}$, and there are at most $d-t\le d$
such terms.
\end{proof}

For $x^N-\rho^N$ with $\rho\ge R$ the skeleton $S$ is the whole polynomial.
In general $S$ keeps the top $t+1$ coefficients and at most $m$ deeper
ones, and on $|z|\ge R$ every term of $E$ is smaller than the top term by
the factor $|z|^{-t}$.

\begin{corollary}[Skeletons at Gaussian roots]\label{cor:gaussskeleton}
In the setting of Section~\ref{sec:gaussian}, let $R>2$ with
$\rho_{\min}\ge R$, let $F\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be nonzero and divisible by $P$,
let $B$ be a block of $F$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussblocks}), of degree $d$, and
let $t\ge0$ be an integer.  Then $d\le\Lambda(F)/\log(R/2)$.  The polynomial
$S\in\mathbb Z[x]$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:skeleton} for $B$, $R$ and $t$ has
degree $d$ and at most $t+1+m$ nonzero coefficients, where
$m(m+1)/2\le\Lambda(F)/\log R$, and
\[
  S(\alpha_j)\in\mathbb Z[i],\qquad
  |S(\alpha_j)|<d\,\rho_j^{\,d-t}\qquad(1\le j\le K).
\]
If $\Lambda(F)\le\varepsilon K^2\log R$ with $\varepsilon>0$, then
$m<\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\,K$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussblocks}, $B\in\mathbb Z[x]$ is divisible by $P$,
$|b_d|\ge1$ and $\Lambda(B)\le\Lambda(F)$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge},
with $\rho_{\min}\ge R>2$, gives
$\Lambda(F)\ge\Lambda(B)\ge d\log(\rho_{\min}/2)\ge d\log(R/2)$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:skeleton} applies to $B$ because $d\ge2K\ge1$.  $S$ has
degree $d$ because $d\in J$.  Since $B(\alpha_j)=0$, we have
$S(\alpha_j)=-E(\alpha_j)$; this is a Gaussian integer because
$S\in\mathbb Z[x]$ and $\alpha_j\in\mathbb Z[i]$, and $|\alpha_j|=\rho_j\ge R$
gives the bound.  Finally, if $m\ge1$ then
$m^2<m(m+1)\le2\Lambda(F)/\log R\le2\varepsilon K^2$.
\end{proof}

So each $\alpha_j$ is a zero of the integer polynomial $S$ up to a relative
error $d\rho_j^{-t}$ against its top term $b_d\alpha_j^d$.  If
$\Lambda(F)=o(K^2\log R)$, then all but $o(K)$ of the nonzero coefficients
of $S$ lie in its top $t+1$ degrees.  This gives a sufficient condition for
the wide-sector bound as a Diophantine statement about polynomials with few
terms, in which neither $P$ nor the angles of the other zeros appear.  Fix $\varphi$,
$\delta$ and $R>2$.  We say that \emph{(H) holds with constants $C\ge1$ and
$0<c<1$} if, for every integer $s\ge1$ and every $S\in\mathbb Z[x]$ of
degree $D\ge1$ with at most $s$ nonzero coefficients, at most $Cs$ Gaussian
integers $\alpha$ satisfy
\[
  |\alpha|\ge R,\qquad|\arg\alpha-\varphi|\le\delta,\qquad
  |S(\alpha)|<D\,|\alpha|^{D-\lceil cs\rceil}.
\]

\begin{proposition}[A Diophantine sufficient condition]\label{prop:skeletonreduction}
In the setting of Section~\ref{sec:gaussian}, let $R>2$, and suppose that
every $\alpha_j$ has $|\alpha_j|\ge R$ and $|\arg\alpha_j-\varphi|\le\delta$,
and that (H) holds with constants $C$ and $c$.  Then every nonzero
$F\in\mathbb Z[x]$ divisible by $P$ satisfies
\[
  \Lambda(F)\ \ge\ \frac{u(u-1)}2\log R,\qquad
  u=(1-c)\Bigl(\frac KC-1\Bigr).
\]
In particular, if (H) holds with constants independent of $R$, then
$\Lambda(F)\ge\bigl(\frac{(1-c)^2}{2C^2}-o(1)\bigr)K^2\log R$ as $K\to\infty$,
uniformly in $R$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $m^*\ge0$ be the largest integer with
$m^*(m^*+1)/2\le\Lambda(F)/\log R$, and put
$t=\lceil c(m^*+1)/(1-c)\rceil$ and $s=t+1+m^*$.  Then
$cs=c(t+1+m^*)\le t$, since this is equivalent to $c(m^*+1)\le(1-c)t$; so
$\lceil cs\rceil\le t$.  Let $B$ be a block of $F$ and $S$ as in
Corollary~\ref{cor:gaussskeleton} with this $t$.  It has degree $d$ and at
most $t+1+m\le s$ nonzero coefficients, since $m\le m^*$ by the maximality
of $m^*$.  Since $\rho_j\ge R>1$, every $\alpha_j$ satisfies
$|S(\alpha_j)|<d\rho_j^{\,d-t}\le d\rho_j^{\,d-\lceil cs\rceil}$.  The $K$
distinct $\alpha_j$ therefore satisfy the three conditions of (H), and
\[
  K\ \le\ Cs\ \le\ C\Bigl(\frac{c(m^*+1)}{1-c}+2+m^*\Bigr)
  =C\Bigl(\frac{m^*+1}{1-c}+1\Bigr),
\]
that is, $m^*+1\ge u$.  If $u\le1$ the claim holds because its right side
is at most $0$.  Otherwise $m^*\ge u-1>0$, and
$\Lambda(F)\ge\frac{m^*(m^*+1)}2\log R\ge\frac{(u-1)u}2\log R$.
\end{proof}

Some precision beyond $|\alpha|^{-1}$ is necessary, so (H) can hold only
with $c>\frac12$ (as stated it holds for no constants at all; see
Section~\ref{sec:Hstatus}).  Let $N\ge2$ and let $\rho\ge10N$ be an integer.  Let
$\alpha$ be a Gaussian integer nearest to $w=\rho e^{2\pi il/N}$.  Then
$|\alpha-w|\le\frac{\sqrt2}2$ and $|\alpha|\ge\rho-1$, so
\[
  |\alpha^N-\rho^N|=|\alpha^N-w^N|\le\tfrac{\sqrt2}2N(\rho+1)^{N-1}<N|\alpha|^{N-1},
\]
since
\[
  \Bigl(\frac{\rho+1}{\rho-1}\Bigr)^{N-1}\le e^{2(N-1)/(\rho-1)}\le e^{1/4}<\sqrt2 .
\]
Consecutive points $w$ are $2\rho\sin(\pi/N)\ge4\rho/N\ge40$ apart, so
these $\alpha$ are distinct.  Rounding moves the argument by less than
$2/\rho$.  So as $N\to\infty$ with $\rho\ge\max(10N,R+1)$, the number of these $\alpha$
in the sector grows like $\delta N/\pi$, while $S=x^N-\rho^N$ has $s=2$
terms and $\lceil2c\rceil=1$ for $c\le\frac12$.  With precision
$|\alpha|^{-2}$ or better the rounding errors must be corrected, and each
further term of $S$ corrects only about one real condition.  A count of
parameters therefore suggests that (H) holds for every $c>\frac12$ with a
suitable $C$.  We do not know whether it does, even for exact zeros
$S(\alpha)=0$.  For those, bounds that do not depend on the degree of $S$ go
back to the gap principle~\cite{lenstra-lacunary}, but (H) asks for a bound
linear in the number of terms.  The exact case would not suffice here in
any event, since the skeleton $S$ is not a multiple of $P$.  Weighted
lattice reduction over integer multiples of degree up to
$2K+\frac{5\pi K}{2\delta}$ found no multiple of smaller mass than $P$.  The
runs used evenly spread root sets and nearly polygonal ones, with
$2\le K\le8$ and $\frac\pi8\le\delta\le\frac\pi4$.  For $K\ge6$ this
$\Lambda(P)\approx K(2K+1)\log R$ is more than twice the linear term
$\pi K\log R/\delta$ of Corollary~\ref{cor:widegauss}.  This is weak
evidence, since lattice reduction does not minimize $\Lambda$.

\subsection{What hypothesis (H) must exclude}\label{sec:Hstatus}

Hypothesis~(H) before Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction} bounds the
Gaussian near-zeros of polynomials with $s$ terms for \emph{every} degree $D$.
As stated it is false for all constants $C$ and $c$: the freedom in $D$, with
or without the factor $D$ in its precision, lets a binomial of huge degree have
many Gaussian integers of one circle as near-zeros
(Proposition~\ref{prop:Hfails}).  The proposition only needs polynomials of
degree $O(s^2)$, because Theorem~\ref{thm:gausscharge} bounds the degree of a
block by its mass (Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletoncap}).  Below this degree cap two
obstructions remain, both from clusters of Gaussian integers at a zero of
high multiplicity.  They exclude $c<\frac12$ in every sector, and every $c<1$ in
sectors that contain a real direction.  The capped hypothesis fails for
$\frac12\le c<1$ in sectors inside an open half-plane as well
(Section~\ref{sec:Hrotated}).

For $M\ge0$ and $0\le j\le2M$ put
$\gamma_{M,j}=(2+i)^j(2-i)^{2M-j}$.  These have norm $25^M$, and
$\gamma_{M,j}=(2-i)^{2M}\beta^j$ with $\beta=\frac{2+i}{2-i}=\frac{3+4i}5$.
Let $\psi=\arg\beta=2\arctan\frac12$; since $\arg(2-i)=-\psi/2$,
\[
  \arg\gamma_{M,j}\equiv(j-M)\,\psi\pmod{2\pi}.
\]
The number $\beta$ is a zero of $5z^2-6z+5$, which is irreducible over
$\mathbb Q$ because $\beta\notin\R$.  It is therefore not an algebraic integer,
and so not a root of unity.  In particular the $\gamma_{M,j}$ with
$0\le j\le2M$ are distinct.

\begin{lemma}[Many Gaussian integers on one circle in a sector]\label{lem:normcircle}
Let $\varphi\in\R$, $0<\delta<\pi$ and $L\ge1$ an integer.  There is $M_0$
such that for every $M\ge M_0$ at least $\lfloor\delta L/\pi\rfloor$ of the
numbers $\gamma_{M,j}$, $0\le j\le2M$, satisfy $|\arg\gamma_{M,j}-\varphi|\le\delta$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Of the $L+1$ points $j\psi$ modulo $2\pi$ ($0\le j\le L$), two, with indices
$j<j'$, are at circular distance at most $2\pi/(L+1)$.  So $h=j'-j\in[1,L]$
has $h\psi\equiv\varepsilon\pmod{2\pi}$ with $0<|\varepsilon|\le2\pi/L$; here
$\varepsilon\ne0$ because $\beta^h\ne1$.  Since
$\bar\gamma_{M,j}=\gamma_{M,2M-j}$, conjugation maps our set to itself and
replaces $\varphi$ by $-\varphi$ and $\varepsilon$ by $-\varepsilon$, so we may
assume $\varepsilon>0$.  Put
$m=\lfloor2\delta/\varepsilon\rfloor\ge\lfloor\delta L/\pi\rfloor$,
$k_1=\lceil2\pi/\varepsilon\rceil$, and let $M_0$ be such that $2M_0\ge h(k_1+m)$.
Let $M\ge M_0$ and $y_k=-M\psi+k\varepsilon$.  The $k_1$ arcs
$[y_k,y_k+\varepsilon)$, $0\le k<k_1$, have total length at least $2\pi$ and
are consecutive, so one of them contains $\varphi-\delta$ modulo $2\pi$.  With
$k_0=k+1\le k_1$ we get $y_{k_0}\in(\varphi-\delta,\varphi-\delta+\varepsilon]$ modulo
$2\pi$, and hence
$y_{k_0+l}\in(\varphi-\delta,\varphi-\delta+(l+1)\varepsilon]\subseteq[\varphi-\delta,\varphi+\delta]$
for $0\le l<m$.  Now $y_k\equiv\arg\gamma_{M,kh}$, and $kh\le(k_1+m)h\le2M$
for $k\le k_0+m-1$.  So the $m$ distinct numbers $\gamma_{M,kh}$,
$k_0\le k<k_0+m$, lie in the sector.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Hypothesis (H) fails]\label{prop:Hfails}
Let $\varphi\in\R$, $0<\delta<\frac\pi2$, $R>2$, $C\ge1$ and $0<c<1$.
In each item below, $S\in\mathbb Z[x]$ has degree $D\ge1$ and at most $s$
nonzero coefficients, and more than $Cs$ Gaussian integers $\alpha$ satisfy
$|\alpha|\ge R$ and $|\arg\alpha-\varphi|\le\delta$ together with the displayed
inequality.
\begin{enumerate}
\item (Degree.)  $s=2$, $S=x^N-\rho^N$ with $\rho=5^M$ and $N=2\rho^2+1$, and
$|S(\alpha)|<D|\alpha|^{D-\lceil cs\rceil}$.  So (H) fails with constants $C$
and $c$.
\item (Degree, without the factor $D$.)  $s=2$, $S=x^N-\rho^N$ with $\rho=5^M$
and suitable $N$, and $|S(\alpha)|<|\alpha|^{D-\lceil cs\rceil}$.
\item (Cluster at a nonreal point.)  If $c<\frac12$: $s=2t+1$ and
$S=(x^2-2ax+a^2+b^2)^t$ with $a+ib\in\mathbb Z[i]$ and $t\ge2$, so that
$D=2t\le s(s+1)/2$, and $|S(\alpha)|<|\alpha|^{D-\lceil cs\rceil}$.
\item (Cluster at a real point.)  If $k\pi\in[\varphi-\delta,\varphi+\delta]$ for
some integer $k$: $s=t+1$ and $S=(x-(-1)^k\rho)^t$ with $\rho\in\mathbb Z$ and
$t\ge2$, so that $D=t\le s(s+1)/2$, and $|S(\alpha)|<|\alpha|^{D-\lceil cs\rceil}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
1.  Fix $L$ with $\lfloor\delta L/\pi\rfloor>2C$, and take $M\ge M_0$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:normcircle}) with $\rho=5^M\ge R$.  The lemma gives more than
$2C=Cs$ numbers $\alpha=\gamma_{M,j}$ in the sector, all with $|\alpha|=\rho\ge R$.
Since $c<1$ we have $\lceil2c\rceil\le2$, so
\[
  |S(\alpha)|\le|\alpha|^N+\rho^N=2\rho^2\,\rho^{N-2}<N\rho^{N-2}
  \le D|\alpha|^{D-\lceil cs\rceil}.
\]

2.  Take $L$, $M$ and the same numbers $\gamma_{M,j}$ as in item~1.  By
Dirichlet's theorem, for $Q=\lceil2\pi M\rho^2\rceil+1$ there are integers
$1\le N\le Q$ and $p$ with $|N\psi-2\pi p|<2\pi/Q$.  For every
$0\le j\le2M$ the number $\theta=(j-M)(N\psi-2\pi p)$ is congruent to
$N\arg\gamma_{M,j}$ modulo $2\pi$, and $|\theta|<2\pi M/Q<\rho^{-2}$.  Hence
\[
  |\gamma_{M,j}^N-\rho^N|=\rho^N|e^{i\theta}-1|\le\rho^N|\theta|<\rho^{N-2}
  \le|\gamma_{M,j}|^{N-\lceil cs\rceil}.
\]

3.  Fix an integer $t\ge2$ with $t>2/(1-2c)$, and put $e=1-2c-2/t\in(0,1)$ and
$u=\lceil c(2t+1)\rceil$.  Then $u<2ct+c+1<2ct+2$, so
$D-u>2t-2ct-2=t(1+e)\ge0$.  For $T\ge1$ let $w=a+ib$ be a Gaussian integer
nearest to $Te^{i\varphi}$.  Put $\rho=|w|\ge T-1$ and $r_0=(\rho/2)^e/7$.  As
$T\to\infty$ we have $|\arg w-\varphi|\le\arcsin(1/(\sqrt2T))\to0$,
$r_0\to\infty$ and $r_0/\rho\to0$.  So for large $T$ we may assume
$|\arg w-\varphi|\le\delta/2$, $r_0/\rho\le\sin(\delta/2)$, $\rho\ge2R$,
$r_0\ge3$ and $r_0^2>C(2t+1)$.  The polynomial $S=((x-w)(x-\bar w))^t$ has integer
coefficients, degree $2t$ and at most $2t+1$ nonzero coefficients.

Let $\alpha\in\mathbb Z[i]$ with $|\alpha-w|\le r_0$.  Then $r_0\le\rho/2$ gives
$|\alpha|\ge\rho/2\ge R$.  The shift of the argument is at most
$\arcsin(r_0/\rho)\le\delta/2$, so $\alpha$ lies in the sector.  Also
$|\alpha-\bar w|\le r_0+2\rho\le3\rho$, and $3\rho r_0<(\rho/2)^{1+e}$, so
\[
  |S(\alpha)|\le(3\rho r_0)^t<(\rho/2)^{t(1+e)}\le(\rho/2)^{D-u}\le|\alpha|^{D-u},
\]
using $\rho/2\ge1$ and $D-u>t(1+e)$.  The square $\{w+k+il:|k|,|l|\le q\}$
with $q=\lfloor r_0/\sqrt2\rfloor$ lies in the disc and contains
$(2q+1)^2\ge(\sqrt2r_0-1)^2\ge r_0^2>Cs$ Gaussian integers, since $r_0\ge3$.

4.  The map $\alpha\mapsto-\alpha$, with $S(x)\mapsto S(-x)$, preserves the
number of terms, the degree and $|S(\alpha)|$, and moves the sector by $\pi$.  So
we may assume $k$ even, i.e.\ that the sector contains the direction $0$, and
take $\varphi\in[-\delta,\delta]$.  The map $\alpha\mapsto\bar\alpha$ preserves
$|S(\alpha)|$ for real $S$, so we may take $\varphi\ge0$; then
$[0,\delta]\subseteq[\varphi-\delta,\varphi+\delta]$.  Fix an integer $t\ge2$ with
$t>2/(1-c)$, and put $e=1-c-2/t\in(0,1)$ and $u=\lceil c(t+1)\rceil<ct+2$.  Then
$D-u=t-u>te>0$.  Let $\rho$ be a large integer, $r_0=(\rho/2)^e/2$, and
$S=(x-\rho)^t$.  For large $\rho$ we have $r_0\le\rho\sin\delta$, $r_0\le\rho/2$,
$\rho\ge2R$ and $r_0^2>2C(t+1)$.  Let $\alpha\in\mathbb Z[i]$ with
$|\alpha-\rho|\le r_0$ and $\operatorname{Im}\alpha\ge0$.  Then
$|\alpha|\ge\rho/2\ge R$ and $0\le\arg\alpha\le\arcsin(r_0/\rho)\le\delta$, and
\[
  |S(\alpha)|\le r_0^t<(\rho/2)^{te}\le(\rho/2)^{D-u}\le|\alpha|^{D-u}.
\]
With $q=\lfloor r_0/\sqrt2\rfloor$ the points $\rho+k+il$, $|k|\le q$,
$0\le l\le q$, are $(2q+1)(q+1)\ge(q+1)^2>r_0^2/2>C(t+1)=Cs$ such $\alpha$.
\end{proof}

So (H) holds for no constants, and Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction}
has a hypothesis that is never satisfied.  Its proof uses (H) only for the
skeleton of a block, however, and the degree of a block is bounded by its
mass.  For $R>2$ put $\ell_R=\log R/\log(R/2)>1$.  We say that \emph{(H) holds
with constants $C$ and $c$ below the degree cap} if the defining property of
(H) holds for every $s\ge1$ and every $S\in\mathbb Z[x]$ with at most $s$
nonzero coefficients and degree $1\le D\le\ell_Rs(s+1)/2$.

\begin{proposition}[The Diophantine condition below the degree cap]\label{prop:skeletoncap}
Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction} remains true if (H) is replaced by
(H) below the degree cap.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $m^*$, $t$ and $s=t+1+m^*$ be as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction}, and let $B$ be a block of $F$, of degree $d$.
The skeleton $S$ has degree $d$.  By Corollary~\ref{cor:gaussskeleton},
$d\le\Lambda(F)/\log(R/2)$.  By the maximality of $m^*$,
$\Lambda(F)<\frac12(m^*+1)(m^*+2)\log R$.  Since $s\ge m^*+1$, this gives
\[
  d\ <\ \ell_R\,\frac{(m^*+1)(m^*+2)}2\ \le\ \ell_R\,\frac{s(s+1)}2 .
\]
So $S$ is one of the polynomials for which the capped hypothesis is assumed,
and the rest of the proof is unchanged.
\end{proof}

Items~1 and~2 of Proposition~\ref{prop:Hfails} need $D>2\rho^2$ at $s=2$,
so the cap excludes them.  The rounding example before this subsection also
has $s=2$ and unbounded $D$.  Below the cap it only shows that $C$ must be of order
$\delta/c$ when $c\le\frac12$, and item~3 supersedes it.  Items~3 and~4 are
not excluded by the cap.  In both, $S$ vanishes to order $t$ at one point $w$,
and every Gaussian integer within a positive power of $\rho$ of $w$ is a
near-zero.  At a nonreal $w$ this takes $2t+1$ terms, and the conjugate factor
$|\alpha-\bar w|\approx2|\operatorname{Im}w|$ costs a power of $\rho$ per unit of
multiplicity; at a real $w$ it takes $t+1$ terms and there is no conjugate factor.
This is where the thresholds $\frac12$ and $1$ come from.  Such clusters
among the $\alpha_j$ are covered by Theorem~\ref{thm:thingauss} only when
$r^3$ is at most about $\rho$, since a cluster of radius $r$ holds up to
about $r^2$ Gaussian integers in an angle of about $r/\rho$.  So the skeleton route can only give the
$K^2\log R$ bound when two conditions hold.  First, the sector satisfies
$0<\varphi-\delta<\varphi+\delta<\pi$.  Second, (H) holds below the cap with some
$c\in[\frac12,1)$.  The count of parameters after
Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction} is consistent with this restricted
hypothesis for every $c>\frac12$.  A Siegel-lemma count also fits it: $s$
coefficients of sizes $\rho^{O(s)}$ can place about $s/(4c)$ generic points
to precision $\rho^{-cs}$.  Both counts miss polynomials in $x^g$, and the
second condition fails for every $c$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:Hrotated}).

\subsection{Clusters in every direction}\label{sec:Hrotated}

The capped hypothesis fails in the case that Section~\ref{sec:Hstatus} leaves
open, and in fact for every $c<1$ in every sector.  Item~4 of
Proposition~\ref{prop:Hfails} places a cluster of near-zeros at a real point.
The substitution $x\mapsto x^g$ copies that cluster to every direction
$2\pi l/g$.  This multiplies the degree by $g$ but leaves the number of terms
unchanged.  A sector of half-width $\delta$ contains such a direction once
$g\ge2\pi/\delta$, so the degree stays linear in $s$, far below the cap.
What separates these polynomials from skeletons is their mass
(Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonmass}).  With a mass cap added, the hypothesis
survives all the examples, but its exact-zero case then follows from the bound
it is meant to prove.

\begin{proposition}[Clusters in every direction]\label{prop:Hrotated}
Let $\varphi\in\R$, $0<\delta<\frac\pi2$, $R>2$, $C\ge1$ and $0<c<1$.  Let
$g\ge2\pi/\delta$ and $t$ be integers with $t>2/(1-c)$ and $t\ge2g$.  For
every sufficiently large integer $\rho_0$, the polynomial
$S=(x^g-\rho_0^g)^t\in\mathbb Z[x]$ has $s=t+1$ nonzero coefficients and
degree $D=gt$, with
\[
  D<gs,\qquad D\le\ell_R\,\frac{s(s+1)}2,\qquad
  \Lambda(S)\ \ge\ g\,\frac{s(s-1)}2\log\rho_0 ,
\]
and more than $Cs$ Gaussian integers $\alpha$ satisfy
\[
  |\alpha|\ge R,\qquad|\arg\alpha-\varphi|\le\delta,\qquad
  |S(\alpha)|<|\alpha|^{D-\lceil cs\rceil}.
\]
So (H) below the degree cap fails for all constants $C$ and $c$, in every
sector.  It also fails if the cap is lowered to $D<gs$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Put $N=\rho_0^g$.  Then
$S=\sum_{j=0}^t\binom tj(-N)^{t-j}x^{gj}$, with $t+1$ nonzero coefficients at
distinct degrees.  Next, $D=g(s-1)<gs$, and $t\ge2g$ gives
$D=gt\le t^2/2<s(s+1)/2<\ell_Rs(s+1)/2$.  For $j<t$ the coefficient of
$x^{gj}$ has modulus at least $N^{t-j}=\rho_0^{g(t-j)}$, so
$\Lambda(S)\ge g\log\rho_0\sum_{j<t}(t-j)=g\frac{t(t+1)}2\log\rho_0$, and
$t(t+1)=s(s-1)$.

Choose an integer $l$ such that $2\pi l/g$ is within $\pi/g\le\delta/2$ of
$\varphi$ modulo $2\pi$, and put $w=\rho_0e^{2\pi il/g}$, so $w^g=N$.  Put
$e=1-c-2/t$, which lies in $(0,1)$ because $t>2/(1-c)$, and
$r_0=\rho_0^e/(g4^g)$.  Since $0<e<1$, we may take $\rho_0$ so large that
\[
  r_0\ge6,\qquad r_0\le\rho_0\sin(\delta/2),\qquad r_0\le\rho_0/2,\qquad
  \rho_0\ge2R,\qquad r_0^2>Cs .
\]

Let $\alpha\in\mathbb Z[i]$ with $|\alpha-w|\le r_0$.  Then
$|\alpha|\ge\rho_0-r_0\ge\rho_0/2\ge R$.  The argument of $\alpha$ differs
from that of $w$ by at most $\arcsin(r_0/\rho_0)\le\delta/2$, so
$|\arg\alpha-\varphi|\le\delta$.  Since $|\alpha|\le\frac32\rho_0$,
\[
  |\alpha^g-N|=|\alpha-w|\,\Bigl|\sum_{k=0}^{g-1}\alpha^kw^{g-1-k}\Bigr|
  \le r_0\,g\,(2\rho_0)^{g-1}=2^{-g-1}\rho_0^{\,e}\,\rho_0^{\,g-1},
\]
and therefore
$|S(\alpha)|\le2^{-(g+1)t}\rho_0^{\,(g-1)t+et}$.  Let $u=\lceil cs\rceil$.
Then $u<c(t+1)+1<ct+2$, so
\[
  D-u\ >\ gt-ct-2=(g-1)t+(1-c)t-2=(g-1)t+et\ >\ 0 .
\]
As $|\alpha|\ge\rho_0/2\ge1$,
\[
  |\alpha|^{D-u}\ \ge\ (\rho_0/2)^{(g-1)t+et}
  =2^{-(g-1+e)t}\rho_0^{\,(g-1)t+et}\ >\ 2^{-(g+1)t}\rho_0^{\,(g-1)t+et}
  \ \ge\ |S(\alpha)| .
\]

It remains to count these $\alpha$.  Let $w_0\in\mathbb Z[i]$ be nearest to
$w$, so $|w-w_0|\le\frac{\sqrt2}2$, and let $q=\lfloor(r_0-1)/\sqrt2\rfloor$.
The points $w_0+k+il$ with $|k|,|l|\le q$ satisfy
$|w_0+k+il-w|\le\frac{\sqrt2}2+\sqrt2q<r_0$.  There are $(2q+1)^2$ of them,
and $2q+1\ge\sqrt2(r_0-1)-1\ge r_0$ because
$r_0\ge6>(\sqrt2+1)^2$.  So there are at least $r_0^2>Cs$ of them.
\end{proof}

The near-zeros form a cluster of radius $\rho_0^{\,e}/(g4^g)$ at each direction
$2\pi l/g$ in the sector, and $S$ has a zero of order $t=s-1$ at the centre
of each cluster, the highest order that $s$ terms allow at a nonzero point.  For
$g=1$ and $l=0$ this is item~4 of Proposition~\ref{prop:Hfails}.  For
$g\ge2\pi/\delta$ the conjugate factor that forces $c<\frac12$ in item~3 is
absent, because the $g$ clusters come from the single real cluster of
$(y-N)^t$ under $y=x^g$.  So the count of parameters after
Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction} does not apply to polynomials in
$x^g$.  The reduction of Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction} needs some
$c<1$, because $s=t+1+m^*$ and $cs\le t$.  It also needs degrees up to about
$\ell_Rs^2/2$, while the proposition gives counterexamples of degree below
$gs$.  So no restriction on $c$, on the sector or on the degree makes (H)
true in a range that Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletoncap} can use.

A skeleton, however, is not an arbitrary polynomial with few terms.  Its
coefficients are among those of a block, so its mass is at most
$\Lambda(F)$.  We say that \emph{(H) holds with constants $C$ and $c$ below
the degree and mass caps} if the defining property of (H) holds for every
$s\ge1$ and every $S\in\mathbb Z[x]$ with at most $s$ nonzero coefficients,
degree $1\le D\le\ell_Rs(s+1)/2$ and
\[
  \Lambda(S)\ <\ \frac{s(s-1)}2\log R .
\]

\begin{proposition}[The Diophantine condition below the mass cap]\label{prop:skeletonmass}
Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction} remains true if (H) is replaced by
(H) below the degree and mass caps.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $m^*$, $t$ and $s=t+1+m^*$ be as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction}, let $B$ be a block of $F$, and let
$S$ be its skeleton.  By the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletoncap}, $S$
has degree at most $\ell_Rs(s+1)/2$.  The nonzero coefficients of $S$ are
coefficients of $B$, so
$\Lambda(S)\le\Lambda(B)\le\Lambda(F)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussblocks}.  By the maximality of $m^*$,
$\Lambda(F)<\frac12(m^*+1)(m^*+2)\log R$.  Since $c>0$ and $m^*+1\ge1$, we
have $t\ge1$, hence $m^*+2\le s$, and
\[
  \Lambda(S)\ <\ \frac{(m^*+1)(m^*+2)}2\log R\ \le\ \frac{s(s-1)}2\log R .
\]
So $S$ is one of the polynomials for which the capped hypothesis is assumed,
and the rest of the proof is unchanged.
\end{proof}

Every counterexample found so far violates the mass cap.  The polynomial of
Proposition~\ref{prop:Hrotated} has $\Lambda(S)\ge g\frac{s(s-1)}2\log\rho_0$
with $\rho_0>R$.  Items~1 and~2 of Proposition~\ref{prop:Hfails} and the
rounding example after Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction} have $s=2$
and $\Lambda=N\log\rho\ge\log R$.  Item~4 has
$\Lambda(S)\ge\sum_{j<t}(t-j)\log\rho=\frac{s(s-1)}2\log\rho$ with $\rho\ge2R$.
In item~3, $S$ is a monic multiple of $(x-w)^t$ with $|w|=\rho$, so
Corollary~\ref{cor:ray} gives
$\Lambda(S)\ge\frac{t(t+1)}2\log(\rho-1)$.  This is at least
$t(2t+1)\log R=\frac{s(s-1)}2\log R$ once $\rho-1\ge R^4$, which holds for
large $T$.  In items~3 and~4 and in Proposition~\ref{prop:Hrotated}, $S$ has a
zero of order at least $(s-1)/2$ at the centre of each cluster.  By
Corollary~\ref{cor:ray}, a zero of order $t$ and modulus $\rho\ge2$ costs a
polynomial with $|f_D|\ge1$ mass at least $\frac{t(t+1)}2\log(\rho-1)$.  This
is the cost that the mass cap charges.

With the mass cap, however, the hypothesis is close to a reformulation of
the bound it is meant to prove.  Suppose that the sector lies in the open
upper half-plane, and that $\Lambda(F)\ge c_0K^2\log R$ for all $K$, all
$\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_K$ in the sector of modulus at least $R$, and all
nonzero integer multiples $F$ of $P$.  Let $S$ satisfy the two caps and let
$Z$ be a set of Gaussian integers in the sector, of modulus at least $R$, at
which $S$ vanishes.  They are not real, $S$ is real, and
$\prod_{\alpha\in Z}(x-\alpha)(x-\bar\alpha)$ is monic with distinct roots.
So it divides $S$ in $\mathbb Z[x]$, and
\[
  c_0|Z|^2\log R\ \le\ \Lambda(S)\ <\ \frac{s(s-1)}2\log R ,
\]
that is, $|Z|<s/\sqrt{2c_0}$.  The exact-zero case of the hypothesis below the
two caps therefore follows from the bound, with $C=(2c_0)^{-1/2}$ and without
the degree cap.  Beyond the bound it asks only for the passage from zeros to
near-zeros of precision $|\alpha|^{-cs}$.

So the skeleton route, as formulated in
Proposition~\ref{prop:skeletonreduction}, is closed in every sector.  Any
condition that bounds near-zeros by the number of terms and the degree
alone fails once degrees up to $gs$ with $g\ge2\pi/\delta$ are allowed, and
the reduction needs degrees of order $s^2$.  A proof along these lines has
to use the sizes of the coefficients of $S$.  Once it does, it contains the
exact problem.  The configurations that the proposition isolates are root sets
made of clusters of radius $\rho^e$, $0<e<1$, around the directions
$2\pi l/g$ in the sector.  Theorem~\ref{thm:thingauss} covers a single
cluster of radius $r$ only when $r^3$ is at most about $\rho$.  For these
root sets the bound $\Lambda(F)\ge cK^2\log R$ is open.

\paragraph{Data availability.}
No datasets were generated or analyzed in this study.
